Performance breakdown

Baseline vs best observed

This comparison exposes latency trade-offs, but tuning a different objective is not the best way to search for the lowest latency. Use a latency objective for that purpose.

Each value is the arithmetic mean of available repeat measurements for this exact workload. P50/P95/P99 are means of backend-supplied percentiles, not pooled request percentiles. Positive throughput changes improve performance; negative latency/failure changes improve performance. Percentage changes are unavailable for mismatched workloads, unequal repeat counts, or missing measurements. Overall means retain the same workload weighting only when coverage matches.

Confidence and validation

Confidence in the result

Inconclusive: repeat measurements show drift.

Descriptive repeat evidence for the optimization metric, per workload. Range overlap is a conservative inconclusive flag, not a statistical significance test. These measurements are not independent proof of production performance. Drift compares the first and second halves when at least four repeats exist.

At least 4 measured repeats are required by the configured confidence policy. Sequential drift of more than 0% triggers finalist validation.

Accepted scores in recorded trial order

This is trial order, not elapsed time. Superseded search executions are excluded.

Parameter associations

Exploratory association across evaluated trials, not causation. For each setting, vLLM Optimizer groups trial scores by observed value and measures how far each group mean is from the overall mean. The displayed percentages normalize those differences to 100%.

How metrics are calculated

Requests/s
Completed requests divided by the benchmark measurement time.
Output tokens/s
Generated output tokens divided by measurement time. Total tokens/s includes prompt and output tokens.
TTFT
Time from sending a request until its first generated token.
TPOT
Average time per generated output token after the first token.
ITL
Delay between consecutive streamed output tokens.
E2E latency
Time from request start until the complete response is received.
Average / median / P99
Arithmetic mean, 50th percentile, and 99th percentile. P99 means 99% of observations were at or below that value.
Elapsed
vLLM Optimizer wall-clock time from benchmark subprocess launch through JSON result parsing; vLLM server startup is measured separately.

The benchmark backend calculates request distributions and percentiles. vLLM Optimizer maps backend names and units into one schema without deriving missing percentiles. Report comparisons use repeat means. For scoring, eligible workloads are averaged within a benchmark execution, repeated executions use their median score, and named benchmark scores are averaged into the trial score.
